## [Unreleased] - v0.89.35

- September 6 compiler-handoff repair: resolve the trusted host executable's
  physical path before constructing a sandbox under a renamed checkout alias.
  Preserve explicit candidate-data bindings and strict candidate/output link
  refusal. Split compiler path, generation and ticket diagnostics; persist
  host-only lifecycle/PID evidence in Engine.AI.Candidate.log so validation
  test windows cannot be confused with admitted comparison previews.
- Review the saved Qwen dock-tab clamp rather than automatically promoting it:
  the existing move algorithm already enforces the proposed bound. Preserve
  the sandbox unchanged and add an independent production-API regression
  covering all 91,520 valid same-group moves, including full capacity, selection,
  metadata and unused-array sentinels. Standalone Release docking CTest passes
  1/1. Renderer-free software-base CTest passes 11/11, including host repo/install
  junction fixtures and interior redirect refusal. Windows Debug/Release editor
  builds and their build-safe aggregates pass through the EpochEngine alias,
  including the actual host-router/path-admission/first-build-ticket contract.
  Source naming passes 593 files and diff checks pass. Exact Release executable:
  12,956,160 bytes; SHA-256
  8e6e4b6e7d8fc009891995da6e3868586e7f368eeec26ec2ae004af727adff12.
  Existing duplicate-logger/optimization-override warnings remain. No model
  rerun, native comparison/succession, publication or stable-ref advance is
  claimed. The operator will test this exact rebuilt executable with full logs.

- September 5 AI usability/continuity checkpoint: finish endpoint-bound model
  memory/default selection in actual Send/Start and detached Chat. Retain
  selected/ejected models across empty inventory; disabled project profiles
  cannot recreate an approved client. A remembered/default Nemotron assistant
  requires an explicit coding choice. Expose coding model settings in the
  self-coding pane. Unknown selected model names no longer fabricate capability
  evidence or cause a hard refusal; independent host context, exact source,
  patch, compiler and validation requirements remain enforced.
- Move completed model reply consumption and automatic initial/successor
  planning to the context tick so hidden AI Controls does not stall work.
  Stop latches automatic progression off and cancels owned model/workspace/
  compiler/test/preview work. Restart waits for retirement and preserves the
  chosen sandbox parent, objective and saved plan while dropping stale request
  identity. Add production-helper regressions for hidden-pane progression,
  no resurrection, six busy/retiring states, unknown models and stale replies.
- Protect cross-context model selection with explicit deferred/worker/reply-
  handoff/MCP lifetime leases, not unsynchronized reads of another context's
  state. Allocate MCP ownership before launch, adopt fresh handles immediately,
  retain failed launch/stop releases, and never stop a borrowed busy handle.
- Separate candidate Editor writable state using its validated private data
  root: startup logs, preferences, cache, project/template output and captures
  no longer default to validated executable/source locations. Core path
  preflight preserves ordinary CRT arguments; candidate paths reject aliases
  and invalid ancestry. This is routing, not OS filesystem/network confinement.
  New renderer-free subprocess contracts cover candidate-root handling.
- Center new/reset Output/AI Chat dividers through one reusable EpochGui
  geometry helper, preserve valid saved ratios, and cancel stale drag coordinates
  on resize. Actual dock layout, drag and preview calculations share the helper;
  the independent bottom-dock geometry contract passes.
- Final Windows Debug and Release EpochEditor builds and both waited
  --engine-contract-self-test runs pass, including model preference/selection,
  iteration loop/session and development-panel regressions. Release software-
  base CTest passes 11/11 in 2.73 seconds. Source naming passes 593 files;
  diff checks pass. Existing duplicate-logger/optimization override warnings
  remain. Exact Release EpochEditor.exe: 12,928,000 bytes, SHA-256
  ea49f941e84540f233bb4185885cb2bb71158c4bd365dff897205b5268408abe.
- Real production HTTP probe against localhost:1234 qwen/qwen3.8-27b:
  first run 21:17-21:21 cancelled request A after dispatch in under 1 ms,
  but B exhausted two 120-second response deadlines (exit 1). Preserve that
  failure. LM Studio recorded a 240000-token load and 35 offloaded layers;
  later inventory reported a loaded instance with 120064 context per slot.
  No model settings/ejection were performed. One repeat after that loaded-state
  observation passed at 21:27:27 (exit 0): A cancelled after actual send, B
  returned its exact canary independently of A's stopped token. An independent
  256-token HTTP health request returned EPOCH_HEALTH_OK in 53.22 seconds.
  These synthetic requests sent no source or project bytes. No transport probe
  remained afterward. This proves HTTP cancellation/recovery, not server-side
  generation stop, GUI/PID comparison, sandbox confinement or two-build Qwen
  succession. Native editor launch remains tool-blocked; v0.89.35/publication
  and the stable baseline remain held.

- Reconcile the planning/documentation authorities with current operator intent:
  real Qwen sandbox build/choice/succession first, current editor-free software
  base next, usable editor/project workflows, then incremental platform/backend
  expansion. Releases gate each accepted milestone rather than the entire
  deferred backlog. Remove the obsolete competing eight-week/two-month plan
  from entrypoints and architecture; retain the playable 2D acceptance contract.
  Distinguish implemented-but-unverified behavior from missing implementation;
  completed evidence stays in history and each active pass retains its exact
  next action. Correct obsolete MCP-registration/sandbox, text selection/tab
  reordering, physical undocking, attachment-presentation and provider-reasoning

  centered-divider requirements without claiming their native acceptance.
  Documentation diff checks and 32 local Markdown link paths pass. No native
  self-coding acceptance, new release or stable-branch advancement is claimed.

- Bind HeadlessCI's explicit root argument to exactly that candidate directory.
  A missing/invalid control contract no longer falls back to a valid parent
  checkout, and candidate validation does not depend on Changes/roadmap.md.
  Preserve no-argument developer discovery using the actual required control
  file. Reproduce the original false pass with the prior Release binary and a
  synthetic missing candidate; its log proves it read the ancestor instead.
  The repaired Windows CMake/MSVC and Linux Clang binaries pass six explicit
  console cases covering missing/invalid nested inputs, valid candidates without
  docs, relative roots and developer discovery, with exact logged roots and
  unchanged input hashes. The opt-in driver retains transcripts; ordinary
  builds and CTest do not implicitly run the new driver.
- Make child-launch ownership explicit: fresh reservations remain owned on
  invalid/spawn-failed launches, whereas focused/busy handles are borrowed.
  Candidate Lab compiler/test workers retain their handle independently of the
  future result, before any diagnostic allocation; completion and context
  teardown recover it even after exceptions or stale artifact epochs. Refuse
  build/test artifact acceptance while retirement is pending. Adopt preview
  handles before identity checks, and retain failed ones through the existing
  retirement pump. Only successful release or typed stale evidence permits
  dropping ownership, not an ambiguous missing snapshot.

## [v0.89.06] - Canonical release staging and project resource spine

- Unify hosted and local package staging on one canonical version reader so
  single-digit revisions retain the zero padding reported by the runtime, tag,
  asset filenames, checksums, and updater metadata.
- Make both release staging scripts infer the source version, reject requested
  version drift before touching build output, and replace silent Linux package
  assertions with actionable RUNPATH, dependency, version, and contract errors.
- Disable persisted GitHub checkout credentials in build-only workflows and
  verify both pinned Ninja archives with stored SHA-512 values before
  extraction.
- Validate Linux dependency provenance against exact build and stage roots,
  including resolved equality for packaged SFML and Vulkan libraries, without
  rejecting the runner temporary directory that owns the valid package.
- Pin the release-producing Linux runner to Ubuntu 22.04 and enforce a
  `GLIBC_2.35` maximum across the executable and bundled libraries so hosted
  packages remain runnable on the supported Linux baseline.
- Replace the accidental `<format>` dependency with the engine-owned
  `core.format` text contract, migrate logger/editor/renderer/update call sites,
  and prove plain, padded, decimal, hexadecimal, pointer, and escaped formatting
  on MSVC and the Ubuntu 22.04 Clang/libstdc++ baseline.

  needed by their exported container operations, and prove the complete engine
  against Ubuntu 22.04 GCC 12/libstdc++12 before hosted package staging.
- Ship the accumulated capability-tier foundation since v0.89.01: explicit
  sampled-image admission, project-scoped texture resources, immutable exact
  Canvas2D resource closure, deterministic texture artifacts, and stronger
  renderer-neutral project evidence.
- Include the temporal morphology spine and preserve product ownership: Plant
  Lab authors reusable tree and forest configurations, while Forest Factory
  remains the standard-editor portal that places those outputs into scenes.
- Include host-owned MCP authority, bounded tool calls, cancellation, evidence
  retention, and operator-selected local model transport without claiming an
  internal LLM or hidden autonomous write path.

## [v0.88.81] - Temporal texture artifact and residency bridge

- Extend `authoring.texture` from identity-only compilation planning to an
  owning deterministic RGBA8 artifact payload with explicit mip records,
  storage-domain layer composition, alpha-aware box-filter generation,
  output/mip budgets, and fail-closed format, color-space, and mip-policy
  status.
- Add full compiled-artifact integrity validation that recomputes identity,
  dimensions, per-mip content, total bytes, and the aggregate payload digest;
  mutated or incomplete artifacts cannot cross into renderer state.
- Add `render.texture.artifact` as the renderer-neutral integration boundary.
  Project-owned logical asset identity remains separate from authoring content
  identity while a one-time validated, immutable artifact seal maps linear
  RGBA8 mip 0 into the existing standalone `render.texture.residency` cache.
- Add build-safe document-to-compile-to-upload contracts covering synchronous
  payload ownership, exact cache reuse, backend-not-ready recreation, backend
  epoch reset, stale handles, revision mismatch, payload mutation, and explicit
  sRGB rejection.
- Make backend-not-ready recreation transactional: allocation, upload, and
  readiness failure preserve the prior cache entry and handle generation; only
  a complete replacement retires the old physical texture. Preserve accumulated
  priority, pinning, and non-evictable policy across that recreation.
- Make ordinary cache-miss eviction transactional as well: plan deterministic
  victims and reserve host capacity before native allocation, then create,
  upload, and verify the new texture before committing any retirement. Failed
  uploads preserve all prior handles, entries, byte gauges, and eviction metrics.
  Bound and report temporary replacement bytes instead of hiding double
  residency during atomic swaps.
- Harden texture residency so backend epochs move forward only and stale
  `resolve`, `touch`, `set_pinned`, and `release` operations cannot disturb a
  replacement resource or release metrics. Charge successful upload bytes
  before readiness checks, and keep artifact copy/move allocation inside the
  immutable seal's fail-closed exception boundary.
- Keep project-registry/Canvas2D resource binding, sRGB-native storage,
  compressed formats, whole mip-chain upload, atlas, bindless, sparse, and
  streaming execution as explicit follow-up work. Runtime artifact schema/read
  ownership without authoring UI and capability-derived admission also remain
  required before a game-only consumption claim.
- Advance development source metadata to `v0.88.81`; keep the published
  `v0.88.69` runtime and updater sealed.

- Define and contract-check the shared clockwise-outward preview convention.
  SDL3, SFML3, DirectX, and Software reject camera-facing back sides before
  projected fill; Vulkan enables back-face culling only for scene solids while
  preserving uncullled line and GUI pipelines. OpenGL and Raylib remain the
  orientation references.
- Remove the unused eager `sf::Context` from shared `WindowData`, keeping native
  SFML contexts backend-owned and preventing pure engine contracts from opening
  X11 during static initialization.
- Force the Unix engine CTest to run with empty display variables so hidden native
  window/context startup becomes a permanent headless regression failure.
- Advance development source metadata to `v0.88.72` while preserving the sealed
  `v0.88.69` packaged runtime defaults.
- Verify MSVC Debug/Release editor builds and engine contracts, the managed Clang
  22 full-engine Release build, and all 4/4 Linux CTests with no display.
## [v0.88.71] - Capability and authoring foundation

## [v0.88.02] - Raylib custom-frame-control presentation repair

- Own Raylib presentation explicitly when the vcpkg build enables
  SUPPORT_CUSTOM_FRAME_CONTROL: EndDrawing() flushes the render batch, then
  Epoch swaps the native buffer and polls Raylib input events.
- Preserve direct-child multicontext docking while hiding only a distinct
  placeholder host, so standalone Raylib windows are not hidden after their
  first rendered frame.
- Complete the epochengine namespace and renderer command-queue source
  migration across the current development tree.
- Verify the Windows x64 MSVC Release static library and editor targets, plus
  the build-safe engine contract suite.

## [v0.88.01] - Raylib native surface and renderer retirement repair

- Make Win32 renderer-owned context retirement one-shot and marshal the final
  ownership transfer to the manager UI thread without retaining an unlocked raw
  window pointer.
- Retire a whole-editor replacement synchronously on the manager UI thread:
  keep the Windows message pump alive, join the exact old renderer, release its
  native resources, and only then allow construction of the new backend.
- Keep editor GUI, chat, and session state alive until the context manager
  proves retirement, and join all remaining renderer threads before final
  context-state cleanup.
- Separate Raylib owner-thread native work from draw commands. GLFW child
  reparenting and resize callbacks now drain before `BeginDrawing`, while the
  ordinary render queue remains inside the frame for actual drawing.
- Replace Raylib's reusable runtime resize closure with one stable backend
  callback. Reused contexts and multicontext windows can no longer feed the
  previous Raylib callback back into itself, and the render thread no longer
  races the UI thread by rewriting `Context::onResize`.
- Initialize logical and framebuffer dimensions together. A completed Raylib
  `EndDrawing()` now publishes first-present readiness directly; Win32
  visibility/parent heuristics no longer suppress editor-session creation after
  a real frame has presented. Resize also avoids redundant `SetWindowSize`
  calls and never revokes successful presentation evidence before the same
  frame can republish it.
- Dispose the retired editor scene, GUI/font, chat, preview, and menu session
  immediately after its renderer joins and before a replacement backend starts.
  Replacement snapshots no longer retain a retired source pointer for a second
  close after restore.
- Restore the shared Raylib frame loop after a switch-specific surface
  synchronization experiment throttled multicontext Raylib rendering. Switch
  readiness remains manager/session-owned rather than changing Raylib drawing.
- Keep a docked Raylib placeholder hidden until the GLFW-owned child replaces
  it, preventing the manager thread from raising an empty host over a valid
  rendered pane.
- Publish Raylib's complete native HWND/DC/GL-context bundle when the GLFW child
  is adopted, and keep logical window dimensions distinct from live framebuffer
  dimensions during resize and frame processing.
- Leave viewport and projection rebuilding under Raylib's framebuffer-resize
  callback. The frame loop no longer forces logical dimensions through
  `SetWindowSize` or a raw `rlViewport` override on every present.
- Copy Raylib texture descriptors while atlas storage is locked instead of
  returning pointers into unlocked hash-map storage.
- Replace the optimized core log bridge's `std::println` and raw `FILE*` writes
  with checked native-console output and an RAII file stream.
- Add build-safe contracts for replacement ownership and restored-frame
  acknowledgement. MSVC Debug/Release editor and StaticLib consumers build,
  and both build-safe editor contract runs pass.

## [v0.87.78] - Replacement transaction ownership repair

- Publish backend readiness only after the renderer completes a successful
  frame, then keep the exact replacement transaction-owned through ordinary
  GUI, font, project, and editor-state restoration plus a render-thread
  acknowledgement of the first restored frame.
- Leave the backend render thread running throughout adoption. A failed first
  frame or failed state restore retires the replacement before the preserved
  snapshot is offered to the recovery backend.
- Exclude routed and floating panel contexts from whole-editor target discovery
  and require existing targets to be live and ready, so a context switch cannot
  adopt a popout or dying context as the primary editor surface.
- Reject every additional switch request while replacement adoption is active,
  including requests that could otherwise find another existing context.
- Add a build-safe ownership contract that distinguishes the active replacement
  context from unrelated live contexts.
- Keep Linux Clang Release optimization at `-O3` generally while compiling only
  `core.commandline.ixx` and `network.core.ixx` at `-O0`, avoiding reproducible LLVM
  22.1.8 optimizer crashes without reducing renderer or engine hot-path builds.
- Serialize active-window, render-thread, and deferred-cleanup ownership during
  replacement. A Win32 close now retires the tracked window first, joins its
  renderer cleanup, and destroys the native host afterward instead of allowing
  Release builds to race an HWND/swapchain against unsynchronized containers.
- Preserve the docking grid's assigned Raylib child position during resize,
  clear Raylib's destroyed GLFW OpenGL context during teardown, and forward
  keyboard/text input from adopted backend children into the shared GUI path.
- Contain backend initialization and cleanup exceptions inside the context
  lifecycle boundary. Failed SDL/Vulkan/other startup now records a failed
  backend and visible log evidence instead of escaping a `noexcept` wrapper and
  terminating the optimized editor process.
- Marshal backend-owned Win32 surface retirement onto the manager UI thread.
  Renderer-window procedures may stop their own loop, but they no longer erase
  active context storage while the editor session thread is reading it.
- Place an adopted Raylib child synchronously on its GLFW owner thread and do
  not accept its first-present evidence until the native surface is visible,
  has a usable client area, and is parented to the selected dock. Raylib also
  clears with the shared editor background color instead of an unrelated black
  surface.

## [v0.87.77] - Render-ready editor session restoration repair

- Remove the replacement-only render-thread pause that could deadlock the
  editor host after Raylib, DirectX, or Vulkan completed native startup.
- Restore editor state through the normal session path only after the selected
  backend publishes render-ready evidence; initialization and GUI/font/project
  restoration no longer overlap.
- Keep the exact replacement context returned by the manager, next-frame source
  retirement, Raylib first-present readiness, owner-thread Raylib docking, and
  failed-backend recovery while making repeated choices use one lifecycle path.
- Add a build-safe contract assertion that editor sessions are never restored
  for pending, initializing, failed, or stopped backends.

## [v0.87.76] - Frame-boundary context replacement repair

- Queue toolbar context changes until the active editor frame has finished its
  GUI and present work, then retire the source backend from the next manager
  frame boundary instead of tearing it down inside the frame still using it.
- Keep the exact replacement render thread at its transaction-owned activation
  gate after editor state restoration until the manager observes native backend
  readiness and completes the handoff.
- Preserve normal processing for every unrelated multicontext window while
  removing the repeated-switch lifetime race and the early hardware-target
  activation left by `v0.87.75`.

## [v0.87.75] - Dropdown replacement transaction ownership repair

- Make the editor context dropdown adopt the exact replacement context returned
  by `MultiContextManager` instead of rediscovering it by backend type.
- Restore the dropdown's editor session immediately after window creation, keep
  that target out of generic multicontext enumeration until it is render-ready,
  and continue servicing every unrelated live context normally.
- Narrow the replacement render gate to session adoption. This supersedes the
  `v0.87.74` post-readiness restore ordering that stalled threaded hardware
  backends while Software continued to work; Raylib retains its required first
  present and the existing retirement, readiness, cleanup, and fallback gates.

## [v0.87.74] - Hardware replacement session handoff repair

- Hold a newly initialized replacement renderer at a one-time session gate
  until the editor snapshot has restored project, layout, camera, preview, GUI,
  and font state.
- Prevent fast hardware render threads from consuming shared camera/preview
  state while the editor thread is writing the replacement snapshot. Raylib may
  complete its required first present before entering the same gate.
- Restore consistent switching across OpenGL, Vulkan, DirectX, SDL, SFML,
  Raylib, and Software without changing backend draw order or the sealed
  packaged updater baseline.

## [v0.87.73] - Raylib owner-thread dock layout repair

- Route adopted Raylib GLFW child layout through its render-thread command
  queue instead of calling `SetWindowPos` from the editor UI thread while the
  context manager window lock is held.
- Prevent the Raylib input subclass from locking against a synchronous
  cross-thread layout call when the user clicks or drags the pane, while keeping
  the stable host placeholder hidden and resize callbacks on the owner thread.
- Keep the published `v0.87.69` runtime/updater sealed; this is a source-only
  repair for focused operator update testing.

## [v0.87.72] - Raylib first-present context repair

- Keep Raylib replacement contexts in `initializing` until their owner thread
  has made the Raylib GL context current and completed a real drawing frame.
- Skip drawing when Raylib cannot activate its GL context, retain bounded retry
  behavior for transient activation failures, and reject the replacement cleanly
  when it stops before first-present evidence instead of entering `BeginDrawing`
  against the wrong context.
- Reject stale Raylib singleton owners and docked initialization without a live
  native Raylib child window instead of reusing a previous switch's context.
- Apply the first-present readiness contract to Windows and Linux hosts while
  leaving the already accepted SDL, SFML, OpenGL, Vulkan, DirectX, and software
  replacement paths unchanged. The published `v0.87.69` release and updater
  remain sealed.

  generated applocal probing, EpochGui manifest discovery, and Vulkan-Hpp
  nodiscard teardown warnings.

## [v0.87.44]
- Hardened updater cancel/retry paths for the launcher and editor: recent
  source-update cancellation now blocks launcher mode switches, active source
  rebuild detection is classified as a source attempt, and launcher/editor
  source-worker monitors fail into visible update status instead of terminating
  the process when stale log or filesystem evidence misbehaves.
- Stopped the release updater from running the legacy CWD-relative `REPO-main`
  cleanup path; source rebuilds now rely on executable-local, tokenized
  `cache/updates/` work roots so repeated attempts cannot delete or race a
  source checkout based on the process working directory.

  packaged updater release while keeping the published stable runtime release
  on `v0.87.42`.

## [v0.87.42]
- Prepared the packaged updater release with a launcher-owned progress surface:
  the normal launcher grid is hidden while an update is active, source rebuilds
  expose Cancel only while the worker can still honor it, and verified handoff
  states switch to Restart with an automatic countdown.
- Restored editor updater restart countdown behavior and kept progress-bar
  status text owned for the draw call so update modal labels stay stable.
- Updated Windows handoff scripts to foreground the restarted Epoch runtime
  after replacement while keeping updater workers hidden.
- Rebuilt the release updater path so a fresh source update cannot reuse stale
  handoff/log evidence from an older worker and fails closed when the downloaded
  source snapshot does not contain the expected vcpkg manifest.
- Added the reusable EpochGui loading-screen layout and routed the launcher
  update panel plus launcher/editor handoff frame through the shared
  progress/loading surface instead of one-off row math.
- Hardened source-update worker startup so stale cancel/log/handoff files are
  cleared before the detached worker launches, stale source roots are removed or
  renamed before extraction, and nested source snapshots are repaired only when
  they contain the required `Engine/vcpkg.json` manifest.
- Hardened updater retry behavior after Cancel or a crash: packaged archives
  now redownload instead of reusing same-URL caches, and source rebuild workers
  use disposable per-run work roots with an active-run marker that Cancel can
  clear without poisoning the next Update press.
- Cleaned launcher/editor update surfaces so checking/cancel states do not show
  a fake `Preparing Update` action, updater log markers such as `[INFO]` and
  `[ERROR]` break onto readable status lines, and launcher/editor loading
  screens use larger replacement/restart wording.
- Moved reusable GUI implementation sources from `Engine/src/gui` to
  `Engine/src/epochgui`, and moved context/backend implementation units into
  per-backend `Engine/src/renderers/...` folders with CMake/MSVC metadata and
  docs aligned.

## [v0.87.26]
- Extended the build-safe Engine Arcade RTT contract across OpenGL-family, SDL3,
  SFML3, and Raylib cabinet graph lanes: OpenGL-family now preserves post-frame
  render-target evidence, SDL3/SFML3/Raylib fail closed when no native runtime is
  live, and Raylib owns CPU-side graph resource records for model submission
  proof without claiming native RTT allocation.
- Tightened renderer capability reporting so System Info and the contract
  harness display sampled RTT as descriptor, graph, hook/adaptor,
  live-allocation, presentation, and rollup layers; SDL3/SFML3/Raylib no longer
  imply presentation proof from no-runtime guard coverage.
- Hardened source-update downloads against GitHub archive-route 404s by trying
  the codeload/API source snapshot URLs after the friendly archive URL fails and
  logging the exact failed URL in both worker and in-process updater lanes.
- Fixed GUI-launched source updates so editor/release update buttons no longer honor
  `EPOCH_UPDATER_SILENT`; silent source-worker exit remains reserved for CLI or
  updater-shell lanes that opt in explicitly.
- Added an OpenGL-first sampled render-surface preview path for Engine Arcade:
  editor/project markers now flag sampled panels, OpenGL renders a deterministic
  attract pass into a native texture/FBO, and the 3D preview samples that target
  onto the staged arcade screen.
- Converted the 3D Scene / 2D Scene/UI toolbar pair into a scene-mode selector
  so 2D game and GUI construction behaves like an explicit mode switch instead
  of another wide toolbar tab.
- Fixed the release updater source-build path so a stale or unavailable vcpkg
  manifest baseline archive no longer ends the update around dependency setup;
  the updater now logs the unavailable ref and falls back to a downloadable
  managed vcpkg toolchain before restoring dependencies.
- Aligned CLI update entry points with the editor updater by passing the same
  platform build-gate metadata, so command-line and GUI update checks follow
  the same Windows/Linux release readiness rules.
- Extended the editor context handoff guard to Raylib so selecting Raylib from
  the toolbar fails closed with visible status instead of entering the unstable
  native owner-thread switch path.
- Fixed the SDL editor context switch path and added a toolbar-equivalent
  smoke hook so source checkpoint validation exercises the same handoff route
  operators use from the editor toolbar.
- Updated release-facing README metadata for the v0.87.26 release checkpoint and
  fixed the EpochGui implementation translation units to include `<cstdint>` so
  the Linux Clang full-engine CI lane can compile `std::uint32_t` and
  `std::uint64_t` explicitly.
- Added the explicit Linux X11 `Window` declaration include in
  `opengl.textures` so Clang modules no longer rely on transitive X11 headers
  when binding GLX drawable state.
- Changed the engine GUI adapter to import the `epoch.gui` module instead of
  including EpochGui headers in its global module fragment, keeping Linux Clang
  symbol binding aligned with the module-backed static library.

## [v0.87.15]

- Kept context switching in-process and non-persistent: selecting a backend no
  longer opens the old Context Driver window, and live-target switching no
  longer demotes the source editor session back to the launcher.

  driver shell.

  focuses/restores an existing live backend context, or opens a new editor
  context for that backend and restores the current editor snapshot into it
  instead of silently faking the switch.
- Tightened context handoff honesty: explicit unavailable backend selections now
  fail closed instead of being substituted, posted native window requests are

  no longer get swallowed as successful switches.
- Documented floating/native GUI hosts as optional editor/tool integrations:
  games, mobile apps, console targets, and headless tools can link the portable

- Promoted `EpochGui` into a C++23 `epoch.gui` module static library with OOP

  pushed the standalone repo setup/module conversion on `main`.

  Validation: MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.

  for wrapped text height before a window exists, closes command menus before
  modal dropdown draw, and keeps the progress lane full-width inside modal
  content bounds. Validation: MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Re-tightened the update modal after the latest operator screenshot showed the
  progress lane could still read as detached and the lower body could look too
  tall. The dialog now uses a smaller dynamic height envelope, centers the
  progress lane inside a capped modal-local width, and shortens the restart
  prompt without changing update behavior. Validation: MSVC Debug x64
  `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Contained the update modal body and action strip after follow-up operator
  feedback showed the dialog could still look broken. Wrapped status text,
  progress, and Cancel/Update/Advanced controls now reserve separate vertical
  regions so the progress lane and button row cannot bleed into each other.
  Validation: MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Bound the update modal's dynamic height so changing source-update status text
  no longer stretches or undercuts the dialog body, while preserving the
  binary-first/source-lane wording and Cancel/Update/Advanced action row.
  Validation: MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Made render-surface material sampling explicit and stricter. Engine Arcade now
  declares the `engine_arcade.screen` sampler in the cabinet material slot, and
  the graph harness rejects a `render_surface` slot whose explicit sampler does
  not match the owning render-texture asset sampler. Validation: MSVC Debug x64
  `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Tightened the update modal's live layout after operator screenshots showed a
  too-large empty body, awkward action row, and progress lane that could read
  as visually detached from the modal. The modal now computes height from the
  same wrapped content it draws, keeps a wider minimum content lane, fills the
  progress bar only inside that lane, and clamps Cancel/Update/Advanced actions
  against the modal content edge.
  Validation: MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Promoted sampled render-texture samplers into first-class graph resources.
  `render.graph` now records the `engine_arcade.screen` sampler beside its color
  texture and render target, resolves the sampler into material read bindings,
  and skips ordinary sampler teardown because RTT asset destruction owns that
  native object. Validation: MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Clamped shared GUI progress bars to their owning content lane and made the
  update/source-rebuild modals fit the live viewport with matching draw and
  input-capture sizes. This keeps update/package progress bars from bleeding
  outside modal bounds and avoids stale advanced-source capture geometry.
  Validation: MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Expanded the Engine Arcade visual proof beyond the placeholder box. Generated
  game-shell scenes that opt into `engine_arcade` now write explicit
  `epoch_editor_entities` for a cabinet base/body/control deck, sampled screen,
  marquee, camera, light, and player start, while the editor package preview
  stages the same cabinet assembly. SDL3/SFML3 graph contract checks now match
  the two-slot body-plus-screen cabinet model instead of accepting the old
  one-mesh proof. Validation: MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Restored the editor stabilization paths that regressed around update/package
  modals, the Video workspace, and single-context project launch. Video now
  stays on the scene-backed surface so the timeline strip is visible, update and
  Package Manager modals share their input-capture/body geometry with larger
  bounded content regions, and Run resolves the generated child executable that
  actually exists in the project output folder before refusing fallback.
  Validation: MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Made Engine Arcade package installation visibly activate the editor instead of
  only staging package evidence. Installing or reinstalling `engine_arcade` now
  selects the default arcade scene, stages an editor-visible RTT screen/cabinet
  proof in `3D Scene`, closes the Package Manager so the change can be seen, and
  lets recognized arcade scenes route through the Run target before project-id
  fallback. Removing Engine Arcade now also clears its preview entities and
  active arcade runtime scene instead of leaving stale state behind. Validation:
  MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Promoted Engine Arcade's sampled render target into a true two-pass graph
  proof. `render.arcade` now builds a tiny offscreen scene model into
  `engine_arcade.screen`, then samples that surface through the cabinet
  material/model pass so the RTT gate proves both render-target writes and later
  material consumption instead of only descriptor shape. Validation: MSVC Debug
  x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Moved broad cross-pass mission memory out of the hot active-pass file and into
  `Changes/mission_cache.md`. `Changes/active_pass.md` now stays focused on the
  current renderer gate, while the roadmap points agents to the mission cache
  for deferred GUI, package, Forest Factory, OS AI, updater, Linux, input, and
  source-shape work.
- Tightened graph-level render-surface honesty. `render.graph` now refuses to
  bind `MaterialTextureSlot::render_surface` from a plain texture handle; the
  slot only resolves when the texture is owned by a sampled render-texture asset
  with a sampler, and the engine contract harness now proves the negative case.
- Wired the real OpenGL sampled render-texture hook factory into the engine
  contract harness. The self-test path now imports `opengl.textures`, installs
  its native FBO/color texture/depth renderbuffer/sampler hooks on the
  OpenGL-family render device, and verifies the shared `engine_arcade.screen`
  descriptor/handle/work-order path without claiming live GPU allocation when
  no OpenGL context is registered. Validation: MSVC Debug x64
  `ConsoleApplication1` passed.
- Fixed the Linux/Clang full-engine build by making `epoch.engine_legacy.cpp` explicitly
  include the public Epoch API type definitions before importing module-backed
  renderer contracts. Validation: `cmake --build --preset ninja-clang-debug
  --target epoch -j 1` completed successfully under WSL/Clang.
- Hardened the Raylib native sampled render-texture device lane for build-only
  and no-runtime validation. `render.device_raylib` now checks the live Raylib
  renderer state before allocating or unloading render textures, exposes
  resolve/count probes for contract tests, and the engine self-test verifies
  the lane fails closed without launching a renderer window.
- MSVC Debug `StaticLib1` builds with the new Raylib RTT guard. The full
  Debug app relink was not rerun because a live `EpochEditor.exe` instance held
  the output executable open; the source change itself is covered by the static
  library and Linux/Clang engine build gates.

## [v0.87.07]
- Promoted graph-level model draw intents into the renderer spine. `render.graph`
  can now attach model draw submissions to compiled passes, resolve those graph
  resources to backend `ModelHandle`s, and submit them through the existing
  command context during graph execution. `render.arcade` uses that path for
  the mini-arcade cabinet pass, and the build-only OpenGL-family contract now
  proves the cabinet material/model binding also reaches `draw_model` without
  launching renderer windows.
- Promoted SDL3 and SFML3 into concrete backend-native sampled
  render-texture device lanes. `render.device_sdl` now owns SDL target-texture
  allocation, bind/clear, restore, and teardown when a live SDL renderer exists;
  `render.device_sfml` owns SFML render-texture allocation, bind/clear/display,
  window reactivation, and teardown when a live SFML window exists. The engine
  contract self-test now verifies both devices build safely without launching
  renderer windows, and MSVC Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` builds with the
  new modules.
- Extended SDL3 and SFML3 through the same mini-arcade cabinet graph contract.
  Both device modules now own build-safe buffer/material/render-target slots
  plus mesh/model records, so the compiled `render.arcade` cabinet pass can
  resolve model handles, bind the sampled-screen model, submit `draw_model`,
  and release the records without launching renderer windows.
- Promoted the first backend-owned OpenGL sampled render-texture hook. The
  `opengl.textures` module now owns native FBO/color texture/depth
  renderbuffer/sampler allocation, pass bind/clear, pass unbind, and teardown
  for the shared OpenGL-family RTT contract; MSVC Debug x64
  `ConsoleApplication1` builds with the new hook factory. SDL3/SFML3 still need
  backend-native target-texture/render-texture hooks instead of pretending they
  are raw OpenGL FBO owners.
- Stabilized the editor command-menu path without changing the protected
  scene/GUI draw model. Legacy launcher/editor loops now use the same OpenGL
  skip-clear policy as the main editor loop, command menus no longer reopen
  behind active modals, and the Window menu is curated back to pane/layout
  actions instead of scene-tool duplicates.
- Extended the renderer-resource spine with mesh/model resource contracts.
  `render.device` now names vertex layouts, index formats, mesh/model handles,
  and mesh/model command bindings; `render.graph` can declare mesh/model
  resources, resolve graph buffer/material inputs into compiled mesh/model
  handles, and tear them down in dependency-safe order.
- Made the System Info surface more honest about that renderer spine. It now
  separates declared graph descriptors from backend-native mesh/model allocation
  so OpenGL, SDL, SFML, Raylib, Vulkan, and DirectX do not overclaim resource
  parity before their concrete allocation/draw paths are promoted.
- Promoted Raylib as the first backend-native model-handle lane. The Raylib
  bridge now exposes stable per-model draw/unload slots, and
  `render.device_raylib` maps `ModelDesc.source_path` into a real backend model
  handle without changing the existing Raylib preview draw path.
- Promoted Raylib as the first backend-native sampled render-texture lane.
  `render.device_raylib` now allocates Raylib render textures for compiled
  render-graph assets, maps the paired color texture, sampler, and render
  target handles back into the shared graph shape, tracks binding-set handles,
  and owns pass begin/end cleanup for future Engine Arcade RTT presentation.
- Added a build-only Engine Arcade render-to-texture contract self-test. The
  engine contract harness now compiles `engine_arcade.screen` through
  `render.graph` with the null device and verifies sampled texture, sampler,
  render-target, binding-set, and pass-binding ownership without opening any
  renderer context.
- Added the first OpenGL-family RTT spine contract. `render.device_opengl_family`
  now gives OpenGL, SDL3-over-GL, and SFML-over-GL one shared logical device path
  for sampled render textures, binding sets, render-pass ownership, and Engine
  Arcade screen-graph compilation before backend-owned native FBO/texture
  allocation is promoted.
- Extended that OpenGL-family gate into the first mini-arcade cabinet graph.
  The build-only contract now routes `engine_arcade.screen` into a sampled
  material slot, screen mesh, and cabinet model pass for OpenGL, SDL3-over-GL,
  and SFML-over-GL logical devices without opening renderer contexts.
- Added the next native-planning seam for sampled render textures. The shared
  render-texture asset plan now records backend requirements for color, depth,
  sampler, offscreen target, and presentable-surface behavior; the OpenGL-family
  device stores that plan per RTT so OpenGL, SDL3-over-GL, and SFML-over-GL have
  one build-proven FBO/texture/sampler work order before native allocation is
  promoted.
- Surfaced sampled render-texture allocation honesty in System Info and the
  compact Systems dock. Raylib is now marked as the active native sampled-RTT
  lane, while OpenGL, SDL3-over-GL, SFML-over-GL, Vulkan, and DirectX stay
  visible as graph-declared/native-allocation-pending until their backend-owned
  texture/FBO/sampler paths are promoted.
- Tightened the OpenGL-family mini-arcade RTT proof without opening GPU
  contexts. The logical OpenGL/SDL3/SFML device now records sampler work-order
  objects beside framebuffer/color/depth objects, and the build-only cabinet
  gate verifies the executed command context binds the RTT material, model,
  sampled texture, sampler, and render-pass dimensions.
- Added the first injectable native RTT allocation seam for OpenGL-family
  contexts. OpenGL, SDL3-over-GL, and SFML-over-GL devices can now accept a
  backend-owned sampled-RTT hook table for allocation, pass begin/end, and
  teardown; the engine contract harness proves that flow with a fake native
  allocator before real GL FBO code is promoted.
- Promoted the mini-arcade RTT graph shape into the reusable `render.arcade`
  module. Engine Arcade screen/cabinet descriptors, material texture-slot
  wiring, screen mesh/model declarations, and graph-pass construction now live
  in one engine-owned module instead of duplicated contract-harness code; MSVC
  Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` builds with the new module.
- Added a Forest Factory preview-spine pass: deterministic Forest entities now
  map to backend-shared trunk, branch, and foliage preview primitives instead of
  generic cubes, and the Plant Lab workspace reports a voxel occupancy estimate
  for future LOD, hit detection, navigation, lighting, and path-trace consumers.
- Added `engine.visuals` as the first central visual-style spine. Frame clears,
  scene clears, object/selection colors, look markers, and editor-only opacity
  factors now route through one module so backend parity can be tightened from
  source instead of per-context color drift. System Info and Console Dock now
  expose the active visual profile and parity gate.
- Added the next renderer-resource spine slice for material texture bindings.
  `render.device` now names material texture slots and command bindings can
  carry material handles plus texture/sampler pairs; `render.graph` can declare
  material resources, bind sampled render-texture slots into passes, and destroy
  compiled material handles through the same device contract.
- Added formal renderer binding-set handles on top of graph resource bindings.
  Compiled graph passes now request backend binding-set handles from
  `IRenderDevice`, bind them through `ICommandContext`, destroy them before
  resource teardown, and surface the core capability in System Info. Engine
  Arcade now declares `binding_sets` and `render_to_texture` only as package
  requirements; RTT remains an engine-owned renderer primitive.
- Bumped the pushed source line to `v0.87.07` for the eye-tested update/editor
  checkpoint while keeping local updater-test rollbacks out of the committed
  source state.

## [v0.87.00]
- Promoted Engine Arcade from duplicated editor strings into the shared package
  registry contract. The Package Manager can now create a game project shell
  from a tool hub and stage the engine-owned `engine_arcade` manifest/script for
  render-to-texture arcade assets, while generated manifests record the default
  mini-runtime scene, scene inventory, render-asset role, and renderer-resource
  requirements from one source of truth.
- Added a backend-neutral render-texture asset plan to the renderer spine.
  `render.device` now describes sampled render-target assets, `render.graph`
  can create the paired texture/render-target resource declaration, and Engine
  Arcade records its `engine_arcade.screen` 512x512 target in package/project
  manifests plus Package Manager evidence rows.
- Added device-level sampled render-target handles and default allocation/
  destruction helpers to `IRenderDevice`, plus an explicit
  `sampled_render_targets` capability bit. Engine Arcade's renderer gate now
  requires that capability so future OpenGL-derived, Vulkan, and DirectX
  implementations converge on the same arcade-screen resource shape.
- Promoted sampled render-texture assets into compiled render-graph ownership.
  Graph compilation now allocates the color texture, sampler, and render target
  as one device-owned asset, maps the texture/target handles back into graph
  resources, and skips duplicate destruction for resources owned by that asset.
- Added compiled pass resource bindings to the renderer spine. `render.graph`
  now resolves each pass's buffer, texture, sampler, and render-target
  reads/writes into backend handles during compile, and `ICommandContext`
  exposes a no-op binding hook so concrete backends can promote sampled render
  targets into material, model, and arcade-screen passes without changing the
  graph API again.
- Added the first central renderer-resource spine: `render.device` now defines
  formal handles/descriptors for buffers, textures, samplers, shaders,
  pipelines, materials, render targets, command lists, and render-pass/
  FrameGraph-ready targets, while System Info reports the active backend
  capability slice for the six production contexts instead of treating OpenGL
  plumbing as the only renderer truth.
- Bumped the active source line to `v0.87.00` for the current feature-line
  refresh after the OS AI/model-package, timeline, package-manager, and
  editor-stability work crossed a larger integration boundary.
- Promoted the old Timeline Editor surface into the `Video` workspace:
  shared `core.time` controls, pacing buttons, playhead status, streaming-save
  cadence, and timeline/video authoring now live there instead of in Systems.
- Scene-backed editor workspaces now reserve a bottom Video Timeline strip when
  there is room, shrinking the scene viewport so 3D/2D rendering does not draw
  behind timeline controls or graph chrome.
- Trimmed Systems back toward its intended role as render/backend/context graph
  diagnostics with a clear pointer to Video for time-spine ownership.
- Normalized the top editor workspace labels to the current operator-facing set:
  `3D Scene`, `2D Scene/UI`, `Assets`, `Plant Lab`, `Video`, `Project`,
  `Intelligence`, and `System Info`.
- Added a reusable GUI `text_link` primitive and moved Package Manager package
  names/status rows away from fake button/details controls while keeping
  Install/Remove/Review Gate as explicit actions.
- Replaced the modal-first update affordance with an automatic editor startup
  update check and a main-toolbar update button that appears only when an
  update is proven available.
- Hardened the packaged updater handoff so Epoch verifies the replacement
  executable exists before launching a self-replacement script or closing the
  editor, with batch-side guards logging missing payloads instead of silently
  shutting down without updating.
- Added a platform CI readiness gate to automatic update checks: Windows
  requires the latest `windows-msvc` job to be green, Linux requires
  `linux-clang-engine`, and queued/running/failing/missing jobs withhold the
  update button instead of offering a potentially broken runtime.
- Simplified the editor update confirmation flow: startup checks still happen
  automatically, the modal appears only for a newer build-proven current
  platform path, the normal smart-update action stays binary-first with visible
  source fallback when no compatible package exists, and `Advanced Source` is
  clearly marked as a source-test path.
- Added visible updater progress/status while the background worker is running:
  the toolbar, modal, and Output evidence now report checking, packaged install,
  source rebuild, elapsed time, and failure/completion state instead of going
  quiet after the user confirms an update.
- Changed update caches to use release/source asset names with version suffixes
  under `cache/packages/`, extraction-verify cached packages before reuse,
  redownload broken package caches, and delete stale source snapshots before a
  source rebuild fetch.
- Tightened updater evidence messages so the editor reports the exact
  binary-first result: platform package found/missing, source fallback
  availability, source worker start/failure, packaged handoff start/failure,
  and platform build-gate blocks instead of generic handoff text.
- Fixed the confirmed source-fallback update path so binary-first installs keep
  the editor open while the source rebuild worker runs, then surface a Restart
  gate only after executable-local handoff evidence says the replacement build
  is ready.
- Hardened source-fallback progress evidence: the detached source worker now
  creates `epoch_update_handoff.log` immediately, mirrors download/restore/build
  stage changes into it, reports errors through the same handoff path, and makes
  Cancel visible to the editor without waiting for the final runtime handoff.
- Hardened Debug-launched source updates: the source fallback now always builds
  the `Release|x64` runtime lane, disables MSBuild node reuse/build
  parallelism, hides the detached worker console by default, and leaves
  handoff-log evidence instead of duplicating console lines or hanging on a
  second visible `MSBuild attempt started` message.
- Fixed the managed-vcpkg source updater blockers where old-CMake `libogg` and
  `libvorbis` ports need generated overlay policy options under modern CMake,
  preventing the editor from parking near the source-worker progress ceiling
  during dependency restore.
- Updated source-update evidence wording to point at executable-local
  `epoch_source_update.log` / `epoch_update_handoff.log` instead of assuming an
  `x64/Debug` runtime folder.
- Hardened OS AI model activation so scan remains inventory-only, the stale
  preferred-model fallback is gone, and the Intelligence workspace plus
  Inspector model pickers no longer share one GUI dropdown state key.
- Added an editor Settings theme selector with Follow System Dark Mode,
  Professional Dark, and Classic Launcher options; theme labels, preference
  resolution, and scoped application now live in `engine.gui` instead of
  editor-local tables, with the system option resolving to the dark tool palette
  until a platform light/dark bridge lands.
- Removed the Linux Clang `game.core.ixx` optimizer workaround by fixing the
  module shape directly: `gamecore` now owns only grid helpers and no longer
  imports atlas modules or exports an unused atlas pointer registry.
- Fixed the `network.core.ixx` impossible-backend path so Clang no longer reports a
  possible fallthrough from `dial()` when Steam networking is not enabled.
- Refreshed the roadmap and release-history archive so the active source line,
  Clang-engine lane, and current OS AI/package progress are recorded in the
  durable change logs.
- Reworked the Package Manager modal from a dropdown-only selector into a
  scrollable package list with per-package Install/Remove/Review Gate actions,
  selected-package detail evidence, and bounded progress/status chrome.
- Trimmed default editor seed clutter: Sandbox, Project Hub, and software/tool
  profiles now start from workspace/camera/light ownership instead of spawning
  starter grids, cubes, player starts, tray panels, or fake tool panels by
  default.
- Removed the last visible Project Hub launcher workspace seed box; the profile
  now starts from camera/light preview ownership only.
- Removed the fake Forest Factory floor/stage object from the scene preview and
  fixed Forest Factory selection to target generated canopy preview nodes rather
  than stale leaf names.
- Refined Systems graph surfaces with clearer render/task lanes, stage details,
  evidence labels, and the OS AI evidence-loop title while keeping the protected
  renderer draw model untouched.
- Clarified OS AI model selection in the editor: model discovery, selected
  model, initialized client state, and manual evidence gates are now shown as
  separate facts, while obsolete watcher/learner controls were removed from the
  visible OS AI surfaces.
- Hardened OS AI model discovery so in-editor model choices persist to
  executable-local `cache/models/selected_os_model.txt`, override stale default
  environment choices on later GUI launches, and stay selected until the
  operator explicitly chooses a replacement model from the detected inventory.

## [v0.84.40]
- Bumped the active source line to `v0.84.40` for the MSVC static-vcpkg
  linker repair and current multicontext/package/GUI source-shape batch.
- Fixed the Debug/Release x64 Visual Studio app linkage for static vcpkg:
  STB image implementation now lives in one private `image.stb.cpp`
  translation unit, SFML shared-item dependencies are skipped when the app is
  using static vcpkg, raylib compiles with `RAYLIB_STATIC`, and the final app
  target carries the SDL3 Windows system dependency set.
- Added `EPOCH_GLAD_PROVIDER=auto|vcpkg|bundled` so CMake owns exactly one
  OpenGL loader per target. `auto` prefers vcpkg `glad::glad` and falls back to
  the checked-in loader; `/FORCE:MULTIPLE` is no longer an accepted GLAD
  collision escape hatch.
- Simplified the editor status strip to show source/build identity, thread
  capacity, active renderer, and zoom inside the editor instead of repeating
  launcher/editor labels.
- Validation: MSBuild Debug x64 `ConsoleApplication1` passed,
  `HeadlessCI` built, `x64/Debug/HeadlessCI.exe` passed from the asset-bearing
  output folder, Windows CMake Debug configured/built/CTested the headless
  target, and WSL Ubuntu Clang Debug configured/built/CTested the headless
  target with DirectX disabled.
- Added the first source-shaped package/core spine for voxel-era engine work:
  `voxel.field`, `voxel.trace`, `forest.factory`, and `package.registry` now
  compile in the CMake/MSVC module path.
- Documented the boundary between core voxel/pathing/tracing and
  package-managed terrain/rendering extensions: Forest Factory is core
  project-opt-in functionality, while planetary terrain stacks, multi-terrain
  authoring, FFT ocean work, and external voxel/renderer prototypes remain
  package candidates under `cache/packages/` provenance.
- Updated the source-shape, module inventory, roadmap, and agent notes so the
  next passes use the Forest Factory name, keep heavy prototypes out of the
  mainline engine, and promote package candidates only through visible
  build/test evidence.
- Tightened the current GUI draw contract by routing modal/dropdown top-layer
  sprites through a backend-independent top-layer batch, then replaying that
  batch after scene preview on SDL, SFML, Raylib, Vulkan, DirectX, software, and
  OpenGL paths so modal windows do not compete with the scene background.
- Renamed ambiguous backend-map locals away from generic `state` in the context
  host paths and package registry research labels away from stale
  prototype-specific names; Epoch-facing metadata now uses renderer-neutral
  voxel research and Forest Factory temporal graph wording.
- Documented that Vulkan source files should migrate into a backend-owned folder
  only through a coordinated filesystem/CMake/MSVC-filter/module pass.
- Added package-registry metadata and docs for optional server-capable work:
  shared network runtime contracts stay inert by default, authoritative
  dedicated headless server support is an explicit package choice, and
  client listen/nondedicated hosting remains a separate opt-in path so
  software and single-player outputs do not inherit server bloat or attack
  surface.
- Pointed bulky optional package implementations at
  `Autodidac/EpochEngineExtensions` so EpochEngine mainline keeps package
  descriptors, security gates, updater/cache paths, and stable API contracts
  instead of absorbing extension source trees.
- Validation: `cmake --preset windows-msvc-debug`,
  `cmake --build --preset windows-msvc-debug --target epoch --config Debug`,
  `cmake --build --preset windows-msvc-debug --target epoch_ci_headless --config Debug`,
  and `ctest --preset windows-msvc-debug --output-on-failure` passed.
- Project Run now refuses the unsafe parent multicontext fallback when the built
  child executable is missing after a build. Successful launches use the Project
  workspace backend selector and pass standalone backend flags to the child
  process; child launches default to standalone OpenGL if no backend is
  specified.
- Added a first-pass `Home` reset hotkey for editor and project-preview camera
  movement, and documented the follow-up input-profile/package system so
  generated projects can opt into the same configurable controls without
  inheriting unrelated editor bloat.
- Aligned Vulkan texture lookup with the existing shared asset-root resolver so
  multicontext Vulkan can find `Engine/assets/vulkan/texture.ppm` from normal
  repo-local `x64/Debug` runs without requiring duplicate loose texture files.
- Routed SDL/SFML parent-grid redock placement through the proxy host command
  path and stopped the proxy redock command from requesting another layout
  cycle, so top-row redocks no longer receive cross-thread parent-grid
  repositioning while their backend-owned host shells are moving.

## [v0.84.35]
- Fixed the hosted Linux/GCC CMake lane by making `epoch_ci_headless`
  genuinely module-free when `EPOCH_CI_HEADLESS_ONLY=ON` and GCC full-engine
  modules are not explicitly enabled. The target now uses a small
  `core_log_write` headless logger shim instead of module file sets, avoiding
  CMake/GCC import-graph discovery failures while preserving MSVC/Clang module
  validation for the full engine and module-aware smoke paths.
- Current work in progress: GUI text clipping, chat focus, project-owned Run,
  and overlay-menu ordering are under validation; renderer frame-order changes
  must not be used as a shortcut for command-menu z-order because they regress
  the hard-won OpenGL flicker fixes.
- Current work in progress: tightened GUI text clipping so edge glyphs remain
  visible without accepting invalid glyph rectangles, latched edit-box focus so
  chat typing does not require holding the mouse button, and reserved the
  centered Run button for active project builds instead of script-asset
  execution.
- Current work in progress: corrected shared GUI Win32 include ownership to use
  the existing platform wrapper, changed Linux/WSL default launch shape to a
  single OpenGL editor context with no automatic WSL Vulkan fallback, and
  documented the voxel/planetary renderer direction as a future architecture
  track rather than current renderer proof.
- Current work in progress: inspected the local
  `vk_cp_cursor_nodoublefree.zip` Vulkan voxel/chunk prototype and documented
  voxel terrain, planetary LOD, path/ray tracing, and procedural plant work as a
  package-gated research track rather than a direct mainline source import.
- Current work in progress: Engine AI self-iteration controls are being
  tightened around a manual-gated evidence watcher, with copy/paste/text-smear
  stabilization and dropdown-based local model selection moving into
  `engine.gui`.
- Current work in progress: promoted progress bars into the shared `engine.gui`
  primitive set, wired Package Manager selection/install feedback through the
  modal instead of Console Dock controls, and reflowed the modal so dropdowns
  do not overlap package rows or bleed progress art outside the window.
- Current work in progress: Project Run now exposes a Project workspace backend
  selector and launches built child executables as standalone single-context
  processes using `--standalone --backend <choice>` instead of cloning the
  editor multicontext shell by default.
- Bumped the active source line to `v0.84.35` for the DirectX multicontext proof
  lane and README screenshot refresh.
- Added the first Windows DirectX/D3D11 backend slice: context registration,
  device/swapchain/render-target ownership, basic HLSL preview rendering,
  deferred GUI replay, command-line/backend selection, CMake/MSVC project
  entries, and multicontext harness coverage.

  while keeping Software documented as safe-launch/debug GUI, capture, and
  headless fallback.

  six-context grid, per-backend startup captures, and operator-provided floating
  context specialty shots.
- Locked DirectX composition to scene-first / GUI-over ordering so command menus
  and launcher/editor GUI surfaces stay in front of the D3D11 scene pass.
- Corrected the DirectX preview pass to honor `ScenePreviewMode::Editor` and a
  valid scene viewport before drawing grid/object geometry, preventing the D3D11
  scene from bleeding through launcher-only GUI surfaces while keeping the
  editor scene visible.
- Fixed the angle-dependent DirectX preview line corruption by clipping whole
  line and triangle primitives before appending them to D3D11 draw buffers, so
  a single clipped endpoint can no longer pair with an unrelated later vertex
  and produce long floating diagonal artifacts.
- Split the first-pass DirectX implementation out of the monolithic source file
  into owned backend implementation units for state/lifetime, device/shader
  setup, preview geometry, GUI atlas/sprite replay, and the public context
  bridge while keeping the existing `directx.context` module interface stable.
- Documented that DirectX is Windows-only and intentionally disabled for Linux
  and WSL builds; the current DirectX source shape is a first-pass backend slice
  and now has real implementation files instead of fake placeholder modules.
- Added the next-pass Engine AI control-loop prompt and acceptance shape so Phase
  5 can proceed from working memory, staged packets, visible execution,
  verification, scoring, and human gates instead of another prompt-only chat
  surface.
- Serialized generated project builds from the editor Run path so rapid or
  duplicate Run/build requests refuse a second build instead of racing
  ProjectLauncher/Sandbox over the shared `StaticLib1` module/PDB/clean outputs.
- Added the same generated-project build lock to emitted Windows child build
  scripts so manual ProjectLauncher/Sandbox builds serialize across processes
  instead of colliding on shared MSVC module/PDB/clean outputs.
- Matched Raylib, SDL, and SFML fallback preview rendering to the OpenGL/DirectX
  Canvas2D behavior by switching their Game/2D camera path to orthographic mode
  instead of leaving those contexts in tilted 3D perspective.
- Centralized preview projection selection in `render.preview_grid` so
  Canvas2D orthographic framing is now shared by editor picking, OpenGL,
  DirectX, Raylib, SDL, SFML, Vulkan, and the software preview fallback instead
  of being reimplemented with backend-local math.
- Routed Raylib dock/undock/redock window commands through the Raylib owner
  thread command queue so the Win32 host no longer mutates the GLFW/Raylib child
  HWND cross-thread during redock tests.
- Staged and version-smoked local release assets at
  `C:\tmp\epoch_release\epoch_win10_x64_v0.84.35.zip` and
  `C:\tmp\epoch_release\epoch_linux_x64_v0.84.35.tar.gz`; the Linux package also

  generated child project's `--project-self-test`, capturing
  `Projects/<id>/logs/project-self-test.log`, adding that log to the staged
  packet, and blocking the gate if the child self-test fails.
- Added an eval guard for rejecting low-evidence helper-model self-iteration
  drafts, keeping weak local replies out of curated training data or source
- Fixed a DirectX GUI smear path where clipped subpixel glyph/sprite slivers
  could be interpreted as normalized full-frame geometry during resize/scroll.
  DirectX GUI replay now treats sprite rectangles as pixel-space only, rejects
  non-finite/empty rectangles, and shared text drawing drops partially clipped
  glyph quads instead of emitting barcode-like fragments.

  Clang engine target, passed `ctest --preset ninja-clang-debug`, and produced

  `epoch --renderer opengl --standalone --editor --smoke --capture`.

  back buffer from the actual drawable double-buffer state. Plain runtime
  smoke without `--editor` can still capture a black frame because it does not
  open the editor proof surface.

## [v0.84.29]
- Bumped the active source line to `v0.84.29` for the OpenGL GUI flicker fix.
- Kept the confirmed OpenGL render order as `scene preview -> command queue ->
  GUI overlay`, then restored OpenGL to the persistent GUI batch so the render
  thread can redraw the latest editor/launcher UI every frame instead of
  alternating scene-only frames between UI ticks.
- Documented the root cause: OpenGL's continuous render loop can outpace GUI
  command production, so queued one-shot GUI commands flicker unless the latest
  GUI batch is retained and replayed after the scene pass.
- Archived the current GUI regression screenshots under
  `Engine/docs/engine/diagnostics/2026-05-17-gui-regression/` and linked them
  from the docs/roadmap so missing panes, duplicate Console Dock controls, and
  scrollbar smear reports remain visible to follow-up passes.

  `Canvas2D` editing camera path. The Canvas2D plane is editor-only, OpenGL

  uses the same orthographic projection.

  bleed during resize/panel transitions. Remaining perspective/launcher flicker
  stays tracked as an OpenGL present/resize bug, not claimed fully solved.

## [v0.83.88]

  expected, and stacktrace availability without requiring experimental syntax.

  `cmake`/`ninja` toolchain in a separate step, and explicitly exports the
  Python tool-shim directories onto `PATH` before configure/build on both
  Windows and Linux runners.

  directly and use `pwsh` on the Windows matrix leg, reducing runner PATH/shell
  ambiguity instead of hoping the right global `cmake` happens to win.
- Repaired the workflow syntax itself by removing invalid `matrix.shell`
  expressions from the `shell:` field and splitting the configure/build steps
  into explicit Windows and Linux branches that GitHub Actions accepts.
- Removed the Node 20-backed MSBuild/NuGet setup actions from the Windows
  workflow, resolved MSBuild through `vswhere`, and made the CI build restore
  the required vcpkg headers/libs into `Engine/vcpkg_installed` explicitly
  while disabling manifest mode for the hosted build.

  honestly: the script host now exposes a queued model-load callback, the
  raylib preview backend can load and draw queued models, `editor_launcher`
  exists again as a real compatibility script, and the generated Sandbox /
  ProjectLauncher child shells now keep that same model-backed bootstrap flow.

  carries the Mini Sponza demo by default, and `ProjectLauncher` is the
  launcher-facing shell that owns the model-backed bootstrap flow.
- Hardened the default local-model selection so Epoch no longer blindly falls
  onto the first detected LM Studio model when an explicit choice is absent;
  it now prefers Epoch/helper/development-flavored names first and avoids
  `qwen` when another detected local option exists.
- Updated the GitHub CMake workflow to set up Python explicitly before
  installing modern `cmake` and `ninja`, which is closer to the real hosted
  runner contract than assuming the right Python environment is already active.
- Added a Windows script-compiler fallback to `cl.exe` so the editor/project
  bootstrap script path does not depend entirely on one specific `clang++`
  install being present on PATH.

  discovered projects should keep a truthful model-backed demo path, and
  `Engine/resource/` remains the canonical Win32 build-resource root until a

  the longer-range six-month/two-year planning PDF in place for now.

- Expanded the editor `Systems` workspace with clearer pacing-health and

- Added naming convergence to the steering docs as explicit debt: legacy `aengine*`/orphan naming and misleading labels such as `multiplexer` are now part of the planned professional cleanup instead of being treated as harmless background drift.

## [v0.83.65]

- Kept the hardware guidance visible in-editor and in docs: the default compatibility target remains 6-core / GTX 1660 Ti-era desktops plus modern Linux laptops, while heavier renderer/lib support stays a project-level opt-in.
- Strengthened the smoke/docs loop around the then-current local LM Studio helper path and proof discipline: launch only from asset-bearing output folders, lean on local helpers for bounded drafts, and treat black software captures as invalid proof rather than a successful screenshot.

- Added the first shared simulation-clock ownership to `core.time`, including fixed-step accumulation, pause/resume, time scaling, single-step support, and editor-facing stats that can travel through the engine instead of living as per-backend guesswork.
- Surfaced the new time-spine state in the editor Systems workspace beside the existing render/task graph surfaces, widened the generated graph textures for broader tooling coverage, and kept the compatibility-tier guidance visible alongside the new pacing/time diagnostics.
- Continued the project/runtime cleanup by removing one more hardcoded sample-launch branch from the older editor loop and keeping the project-driven play path centered on real scene ids.
- Updated README, runtime/build/smoke/AI docs around the time-based engine direction, the GPU-driven baseline renderer plan, the later SpeedTree-like procedural/node authoring phase with future O2L integration, and the helper-first workflow that can use up to four parallel prompts per loaded local helper model.

## [Unreleased]
- Split hosted validation so required Windows and Linux CMake lanes use the
  portable `epoch_ci_headless` compile/run smoke, while full vcpkg-backed
  graphics builds remain local/release validation until hosted graphics
  dependencies are hardened.
- Added the `HeadlessCI` Visual Studio smoke project and shared
  `src/ci/headless_smoke.cpp` to verify the script-host ABI and path probes
  without importing the graphics shared items.
- Hardened the Windows OpenGL module fragments with a local WGL extension
  fallback so CMake/MSVC builds no longer require `GL/wglext.h` just to scan the
  OpenGL sources.
- Added seed-object, archetype-type, category-mix, and visibility proof rows to
  the editor Project workspace so the primitive/object system now has a
  truthful in-editor baseline instead of living only in scene seed code.
- Tightened the cross-platform CMake workflow for GitHub Actions by switching
  the Windows configure/build path to PowerShell and installing an explicit
  modern CMake + Ninja toolchain before configure so hosted CL/MSVC runs stop
  depending on runner defaults.
- Removed the duplicate Win32 multicontext bring-up path so context layout and render-thread startup only happen once during engine/editor launch instead of being re-fired from both `Initialize()` and the outer startup loop.
- Added a short per-context startup stagger on both Win32 and Linux multicontext managers so heavy backends do not all initialize on the same instant, which reduces random bring-up stalls and gives the editor a calmer startup path.
- Stopped OpenGL from swapping an unready first frame while its quad pipeline is still unavailable, reducing the intermittent load-time flicker/blank-present seam during startup.
- Switched Win32 SDL/SFML proxy drags to child-relative screen math and kept proxy panes detached until mouse release, so parented top-row drags stop snap-redocking mid-drag and stop picking up host-frame drift.
- Extended the parented Win32 harness to record the proxy release-redock contract explicitly (`ContractMode`, `LateDetached`, `EndRedocked`) instead of pretending proxy-child panes should redock live like direct-child panes.
- Revalidated focused serial SDL and SFML parented drag cycles against the new contract and kept the close/destroy multiplexer logging visible for user-close vs orderly shutdown diagnosis.
- Added small shared GUI clip slack to reduce first-glyph clipping on left-edge buttons and text rows without touching launcher palette choices.
- Tightened the Project and AI workspaces with clearer play/build readiness rows, exact generated project path logging, and clearer staged-vs-curated AI role/evidence messaging.
- Split the Windows parented multicontext harness into two honest proof modes: normal serial runs keep dock/undock evidence clean, while the new opt-in `-CaptureStartupProof` path records startup image samples for first-frame rendered-content checks without replacing the drag baseline.
- Revalidated focused single-backend SDL and SFML dock -> undock -> redock truth after the harness split, and captured separate startup-proof runs that now report non-empty rendered-content samples for both backends.
- Added a small shared GUI text inset so left-edge buttons stop clipping the first glyph against the content clip boundary.
- Tightened the parented Win32 multicontext harness so drag proofs poll for real undock/redock state transitions instead of assuming a fixed 250 ms sample window, which was under-reporting SFML full-grid escape/return behavior.
- Confirmed with new Debug evidence that SFML full-grid runs can now show real `Posted Undock -> Posted Redock -> Redock begin/end` sequencing in the engine log, while focused SDL/SFML single-backend runs remain green.
- Added more in-editor project proof rows for generated project shells and script sources so the Project and Scripts workspaces expose concrete file/output/log locations rather than only status text.
- Fixed the Win32 SFML redock decision to use the actual mouse-release screen point instead of stale drag history, which was leaving some full-grid runs detached even when the release returned inside the parent window.
